# Android third-party notices

Generated by `scripts/android/prepare-license-notices.js` from pinned
sources; ordinary builds package this committed file unchanged. Pins:
Sherpa 142807252687d81b40d6315f23470a1512a00de3, ORT 8f0278c77bf44b0cc83c098c6c722b92a36ac4b5, ORT AAR com.microsoft.onnxruntime:onnxruntime-android:1.27.0 sha256 077dec5e2d821234c7dc0aba584bec8f999854b546c754cab93a90741c56fbeb,
Tesseract acffef2b66eb44a31df297e11d905f4b39001068, PDF.js 0365cbde028bd92e58f2dab1bb70cd30ac7acfd7.

## Combined work

This Android application incorporates eSpeak NG and Unicode tools through
Sherpa ONNX/Piper phonemization. These components carry GPLv3 terms, with
later-version permission where granted. The combined distribution is therefore
declared GPL-3.0-only in F-Droid metadata and must satisfy applicable GPL
terms. Papercut's original source retains MIT notices; other dependencies
retain their notices. Matching corresponding source and build instructions
accompany each release.

## Models and voices

- Piper Kareem (Arabic voice): disclosure — the pinned archive's model card
  points dataset licensing to AliMokhammad/arabicttstrain, which cannot be
  retrieved, and records fine-tuning from the lessac voice; repository MIT
  metadata does not settle these rights. Kept as an optional download with
  this status disclosed in the listing, pending an upstream rights statement.
- Supertonic 3: the pinned archive distinguishes MIT sample code from
  OpenRAIL-M weights that carry use restrictions. Kept as an optional download
  and declared with the F-Droid NonFreeAssets anti-feature.
- Kokoro: Apache-2.0 archive; its bundled eSpeak data/dictionaries and
  CppJieba dictionaries are covered by the GPL and MIT notices above.
- E5 embeddings (Small/Base/Large): all pinned model cards declare MIT; the
  MIT text above covers them; hash-pinned ONNX/tokenizer assets keep per-file
  notices.
- English and Arabic OCR trained data byte-match naptha/tessdata 806cd9adc8c6e8abc11c782db1818c990576bebc and carry
  its Apache-2.0 source license, stated separately from the npm wrappers'
  MIT metadata.

## Rust, JavaScript and Maven dependencies

Per-package attributions below come from the pinned lockfiles and package
metadata; Maven dependencies (AndroidX, Material, CameraX, the ONNX Runtime
AAR and others) are declared in their POMs, and the resolved graph is
auditable with the standard refresh commands recorded in the source
inventory. License texts shared verbatim across packages are printed once
with the packages grouped beneath them.

## Corresponding source

Each release ships the exact pinned source revisions, patches, build scripts
and this notice as a corresponding-source archive with build instructions, so
the GPL terms of the combined distribution are satisfied.

## Piper Kareem model card

Piper Kareem model card (verbatim, from the pinned archive):

# Model card for kareem (medium)

* Language: ar_JO (Arabic, Jordan)
* Speakers: 1
* Quality: medium
* Samplerate: 22,050Hz

## Dataset

* URL: https://github.com/AliMokhammad/arabicttstrain/
* License: See URL

## Training

Finetuned from U.S English lessac voice (medium quality).

https://github.com/aappleby/smhasher

SMHasher is a test suite designed to test the distribution, collision, and
performance properties of non-cryptographic hash functions.
This is the home for the MurmurHash family of hash functions along with the
SMHasher test suite used to verify them.

SQLite Is Public Domain

All of the code and documentation in SQLite has been dedicated to the public
domain by the authors. All code authors, and representatives of the companies
they work for, have signed affidavits dedicating their contributions to the
public domain and originals of those signed affidavits are stored in a firesafe
at the main offices of Hwaci. Anyone is free to copy, modify, publish, use,
compile, sell, or distribute the original SQLite code, either in source code
form or as a compiled binary, for any purpose, commercial or non-commercial,
and by any means.

The previous paragraph applies to the deliverable code and documentation in
SQLite - those parts of the SQLite library that you actually bundle and ship
with a larger application. Some scripts used as part of the build process (for
example the "configure" scripts generated by autoconf) might fall under other
open-source licenses. Nothing from these build scripts ever reaches the final
deliverable SQLite library, however, and so the licenses associated with those
scripts should not be a factor in assessing your rights to copy and use the
SQLite library.

All of the deliverable code in SQLite has been written from scratch. No code
has been taken from other projects or from the open internet. Every line of
code can be traced back to its original author, and all of those authors have
public domain dedications on file. So the SQLite code base is clean and is
uncontaminated with licensed code from other projects.

## OpenLibm

OpenLibm contains code that is covered by various licenses.

The OpenLibm code derives from the FreeBSD msun and OpenBSD libm
implementations, which in turn derives from FDLIBM 5.3. As a result, it
has a number of fixes and updates that have accumulated over the years
in msun, and also optimized assembly versions of many functions. These
improvements are provided under the BSD and ISC licenses. The msun
library also includes work placed under the public domain, which is
noted in the individual files. Further work on making a standalone
OpenLibm library from msun, as part of the Julia project is covered
under the MIT license. The test files, test-double.c and test-float.c
are under the LGPL.
